<meta>This page is the opening of the preface to J. G. Toelten’s alchemical work. It establishes the text's provenance as a formerly secret manuscript held by the Rosicrucians. The catchword "und" (and) at the bottom indicates the sentence continues on the following page.</meta>

### Honored Reader,
### According to Rank and Merit!

Here is brought to the light of day the ***Chymical Heaven Unlocked*** <note>original: *Coelum reseratum Chymicum*</note> of the world-famous philosopher **J. G. Toelten**. This work has lain hidden for many years—indeed, for more than a full **century** <note>original: *seculum*</note>—as a manuscript. It was kept first within the famous **Fraternity of the Rosy Cross** <note>original: *Fraternität Roseæ Crucis*; a legendary secret society of sages and alchemists known as the Rosicrucians.</note>, and subsequently, for a short time, it remained concealed in the hands of a few devotees of this **<term>Noble Art</term>** <note>A traditional name for alchemy, emphasizing its spiritual and physical refinement of matter.</note>. Although the **author** intended for it to be sent to the press in **Nuremberg** and shared with the world even then, the aforementioned highly esteemed Fraternity did not find it useful or advisable at that time for such **<term>Secrets</term>** <note>original: *arcana*; refers to hidden or specialized medicinal and alchemical knowledge.</note> to be spread so freely into the world for pious

<summary>This preface introduces J. G. Toelten’s alchemical treatise, revealing that it was preserved as a secret Rosicrucian manuscript for over a hundred years. It explains that while the author originally intended to publish it in Nuremberg, the Rosicrucian brotherhood initially withheld it from the public.</summary>
<keywords>J. G. Toelten, Alchemy, Rosicrucians, Coelum Reseratum Chymicum, Nuremberg, secret knowledge, Arcana</keywords>

-># Preface.<-

you will find the most precious **medicines** <note>original: *Artzeneyen*; in alchemical literature, this refers both to physical cures and the spiritual "Panacea" for the soul.</note> for mankind. In this regard, I cannot help but mention what a certain expert and admirer of **<term>Hermetic Philosophy</term>** <note>A tradition of spiritual and alchemical knowledge seeking the perfection of both matter and the human soul.</note> and its works recently revealed to me in response when I showed him this manuscript and my intention to make it known to the world through printing. He replied: that there were things contained within it that the wicked world (the pious excepted) was not worthy of having made known to them; however, we are now living in the **final days** <note>original: *letzten Zeiten*; many 17th and 18th-century alchemists believed they were living in an apocalyptic age where secret knowledge would finally be revealed.</note>, about which the ancient philosophers long ago prophesied that **<term>Elias Artista</term>** <note>A legendary messianic figure in alchemy, first mentioned by Paracelsus, who was expected to appear at the end of time to reveal the final secrets of nature.</note> would come and discover for the world everything that has been secret and hidden until now; perhaps this book is his forerunner. Furthermore, it is known that God, according to His inscrutable wisdom, holds the highest **governance** <note>original: *Directorium*</note> over everything in the world; thus it would also be possible for Him to hold His strong hand of omnipotence over this work to shield it from wicked men, and to punish them with blindness just as happened to the **Sodomites** <note>A biblical reference to Genesis 19, where the men of Sodom were struck with blindness so they could not find the door to Lot's house.</note>, so that they cannot find the door of the pious **Lot**, and thus they might not be able to enjoy it, since

# THE UNLOCKED CHEMICAL HEAVEN. <note>original: *COELVM RESERATUM CHYMICVM*</note>

As it was translated by the **Brotherhood of the Rose Cross**, philosophically and cabalistically <note>original: *Philosophicè und Cabalisticè*; "Cabalistically" refers to the Christian Kabbalah, a method of interpreting the divine through the symbolism of letters and numbers, often used by alchemists to decode the secrets of nature.</note> with all the associated manual procedures; nothing has been withheld that is necessary for the Art <note>the "Art" refers to the Great Work of alchemy: the perfection of both metals and the human soul.</note>, prescribed for the complete instruction of those students who are accepted into the Brotherhood; this is revealed to them word for word, and no one shall ~~not~~ <note>The original text contains a struck-through word, correcting a double negative in the German sentence.</note> receive this to read unless he has first performed his proper duties, or is in the process of performing them. Written and translated into German. Dordrecht, May 12, 1612.

## Address to the Students of the Art.

<margin>
le,

</margin>
<note>These marginal fragments appear to be remnants of words cut off or damaged at the edge of the manuscript, possibly intended to be "alle" (all) or "ich" (I).</note>
Dear sons and successors in our **Art** <note>In this context, "the Art" refers to alchemy or the "Great Work" of spiritual and physical transformation.</note>, you should know that we hereby hand over to you this secret **Wisdom**, and we tell you by the highest truth, which is **God** Himself: if you do not apply this to the glory of **God**, if you let such things reach the wicked and the unrepentant, or if you are helpful to them with it, or use it for gruesome bloodshed, then believe for certain that the hand of the **Lord** will strike you in an instant. This has happened to many who have done such things; therefore, keep the **Lord** your **God** before your eyes and in your heart, and you will see all His wonders, which can be described by no pen, nor can any tongue express them. Afterwards, you will gladly renounce the world and its things, and hold this **Wisdom** higher than all things of this world—especially if you receive the following **Wisdom** from the **Lord** your **God** in this life. Therefore, purify your hearts, and then pray to **God** that He would let you grow and increase in **Wisdom**, to work out these aforementioned things bit by bit. You can also make for yourselves a daily **perpetual cycle of Nature** <note>original: *perpetuum Naturæ*; likely referring to a "philosophical" device or alchemical process that mimics the self-sustaining rotations of the heavens and earth.</note>, in which all four seasons show themselves, as well as the darkness of night and the light of day, through the concentrated

He promises that those who purify their hearts will witness indescribable wonders and a "perpetual cycle of nature" that reflects the seasons and light.</summary>

<keywords>alchemical art, secret wisdom, divine retribution, purification of heart, perpetual nature, seasons, moral responsibility</keywords>

--- Page 18 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Address to the Students," transitioning from the prayer for wisdom on the previous page to a description of the alchemical Microcosm and the origins of the following text. The catchword from the previous page was "Vor," likely indicating the start of the "Vorrede" (Preface) or "Vornehmste" (Most Noble) secrets.</meta>

<image-desc>Decorative typographical header at the top of the page consisting of fleurons, colons, parentheses, and a small circle, arranged symmetrically.</image-desc>

Nature: by day the sun, and by night the moon and stars. Just as there are clouds, dew, air, and rain in the great world <note>The "Great World" refers to the Macrocosm, or the universe.</note>, so these shall also manifest in the small world <note>The "Small World" refers to the Microcosm—the alchemical vessel or the human being—which mirrors the universe.</note>. Mountains and valleys, foliage and grass, and the little flowers will bear small fruits. All kinds of animals will appear, as well as clear waters with living creatures. And just as the seasons change outwardly in the great world, so too will this happen in your small world. This is the true **perpetual motion** <note>original: *perpetuum mobile*</note>. Included herewith are the writings of the famous **Toelten**, called **The Unlocked Heaven** <note>original: *Cœlum reseratum*</note>, which have never been printed because they were bought up while still in the press in Nuremberg. Subsequently, however, they were translated by us into German, with all the practical instructions and necessary remarks added word for word. All the works from **minerals**, **metals**, **animals**, and **vegetables** are included, just as I have not only described the "chaotic" and "astral" works from the wondrous materials with all the practical maneuvers, and how to make the highest **tincture** <note>A potent alchemical substance used for transmutation or healing.</note> from each; but also how one should make pearls, **carbuncles** <note>term: *Carfunckel*; a historical name for any deep red gemstone, particularly garnets or rubies.</note>, diamonds, rubies, and all other stones, as good as and even better than those made by nature. All of this you shall apply to the honor of the great **Creator**, and always give thanks to Him with great reverence, through Christ our Lord.

->**Johann Carl von Friesau,**<-
->J. F. R. C.<- <note>Likely standing for "Jesus, Frater Rosae Crucis" (Jesus, Brother of the Rose Cross), signaling Rosicrucian affiliation.</note>

<summary>This page concludes the author's address, explaining that the alchemical process mirrors the natural world (microcosm vs. macrocosm) and identifying the text as a translation of Toelten's suppressed work on creating tinctures and gemstones.</summary>

## Preface of Toeltius. <note>Johann Toeltius was the pseudonymous author of *Coelum Reseratum Chymicum* (The Chemical Heaven Unlocked), first published in the early 18th century.</note>

Dear Reader, I was not in the be-
ginning inclined to allow the
present little work to go into
print; however, because ma-
ny have slandered my previous
little treatises, especially the *Chemical Light* <note>original: *Lumen Chymicum*</note>,
as if they were nothing but fantasies, with which
I sought something extraordinary from the world,
and thereby this noble art—which is nonetheless
truthful—is decried as the Devil’s delusion <term>delusion: *Blendwerck*, literally "blinding-work," referring to a deceptive trick or a demonic mirage</term>,
but one should read the entire Epistle of Jude
concerning this sort of people; for whatever such
people know by natural means, in those things
they perish like cattle, for the word of the Lord
has become a mere trade <term>trade: *Handthierung*, meaning a craft, business, or manual handling</term> to them,
which they have adopted according to their spirit-
less mind, so that they may lead the simple
away from the truth and toward their master, Lu-
cifer, and so that they might be worshipped as
gods by the simple,
gaining a great following <note>original: *Zulauff*</note>, but these

<summary>In this preface, the author Johann Toeltius defends his alchemical writings against critics who dismiss his work as fantasy or demonic deception. # How the Philosopher’s Stone is to be made in various ways from minerals and metals.

## Chapter I.

<margin>
Work from Antimony and Mars, by Basil Valentine and Alexander von Suchten, etc. <note>original: *Opus ex Antimonio et Marte, Basilii Valentini, Suchtenii p*; Basilius Valentinus was a legendary 15th-century alchemist, and Alexander von Suchten was a 16th-century physician known for his work on the medical uses of antimony.</note>
</margin>

So I begin to write in the name of the great **GOD**, revealing these great secrets openly and clearly; therefore know that the ancients had no glassware, yet they truly made the Master of the Philosopher’s Stone. I will thus begin by describing the oldest works, which are the shortest and proceed from the metallic and mineral kingdoms. Therefore, see to it that you obtain a proper <term>Hungarian Antimony ore</term> <note>original: *minera Antimonii Hungarici*; Hungarian antimony was highly prized by alchemists for its perceived purity and high sulfur content, essential for the "Star Regulus" process.</note>.

<margin>
Saturnine Material <note>original: *Materia Saturnina*; in alchemy, Saturn is the planetary correspondence for lead, but here it refers to the heavy, lead-like appearance of antimony ore.</note>
</margin>

Take 1 pound of such <term>Saturnine material</term> and crush it into small pieces. First, let 16 <term>Loth</term> <note>A historical unit of weight; one Loth is approximately 15-16 grams or half an ounce.</note> of good <term>Hüttenberg steel</term> <note>Steel from the Hüttenberg region in Austria, which was famous for its high-quality iron ore.</note> be heated to a white-hot weld in a good <term>crucible</term> <note>original: *Tiegel*; a ceramic or graphite container used for melting materials at very high temperatures.</note>. Then mix the above-mentioned antimony with half a quarter-pound of white <term>tartar</term> <note>original: *Wein-Stein*; literally "wine-stone," the crystalline crust that forms in wine barrels (potassium bitartrate), used here as a flux to help the metal flow.</note>, and cast these together onto the iron in the crucible. When everything is flowing well, then also add a quarter-pound of <term>saltpeter</term> <note>Potassium nitrate, used as an oxidizing agent to help separate the metallic part of the antimony from the waste rock (slag).</note> to it,

<margin>Saturnine Matter <note>In alchemy, 'Saturn' usually refers to lead, but here it refers to the heavy, mineral nature of the antimony ore which shares lead's characteristics.</note></margin> Take 1 pound of such Saturnine matter, and crush it small; first let 16 <term>Loth</term> <note>A historical German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to half an ounce or 15 grams.</note> of good <term>Hüttenberg steel</term> <note>Steel from the Hüttenberg region in Carinthia, known for its high quality and purity.</note> be welded in a good crucible, then mix into the above antimony half a quarter-pound of white <term>tartar</term> <note>original: *Wein-Stein*; potassium bitartrate, a substance collected from wine barrels used here as a flux to help the metals melt together.</note>, and add this together onto the iron in the crucible; when everything flows well, then also add a quarter-pound of saltpeter upon it,

...other <note>This completes the word from the previous page's catchword "and-".</note>, so the snake <note>An alchemical metaphor likely referring to a corrosive solvent or a specific mercurial substance that "swallows" or reacts with the base material.</note> will dissolve the <term>glass</term> <note>original: *Vitrum*</note> and swallow it into itself, and a blood-red <term>salt</term> will come of it. The blood-red <term>salt</term> that has fallen to the bottom should be well sweetened <note>original: *aus süssen*; the process of washing a precipitate with water to remove any remaining salts or acids.</note> with distilled or filtered rainwater, and then dried. This flows like a fat in the fire; thus you have the red and white flower.

By the 6th or 7th time, all your **material** <note>original: *Materia*</note> will have risen and come over like a **blood-red oil** <note>original: *Blut-rothes Oel*</note>. Pour it back once more and distill it over. If it is made from purified **sulfur** <note>original: *Schwefel*</note>, it is finished; however, if it is made from the red **glass** <note>original: *vitro*; likely referring to the "glass of antimony," a translucent red substance produced by melting antimony ore.</note>, it must be **rectified** <note>original: *rectificiret*; a process of refining a liquid by repeated distillation to increase its purity and potency.</note> at least 4 more times. Even then, it is not as good as the other, though it still produces a powerful **tincture** <note>original: *Tinctur*; in alchemy, a medicine or substance capable of transforming or "dyeing" other bodies.</note>. Now take your oil, which will be entirely fixed and fire-resistant. Pour an equal amount of distilled **rainwater** <note>original: *Regen-Wasser*; often used in alchemy as a pure source of "heavenly" water.</note> into it and place it in a gentle **water bath** <note>original: *Balneum*; specifically a *Balneum Mariae*, used for controlled, indirect heating.</note> to digest. Then the oil will settle to the bottom, pale-red and glowing like a **carbuncle** <note>original: *Carfunckel*; a legendary red gemstone said to emit its own light; here describing the brilliant clarity and color of the oil.</note>, and the salty **lye** <note>original: *Lixivium*</note> will remain on top. This is poured off, and fresh **rainwater** is added again so that all the salt may be drawn out. When the water no longer tastes of salt, pour everything together into a **flask** <note>original: *Kolben*</note> and distill the water off again until only the **liquid** <note>original: *liqvor*</note> remains, which can be kept for further use.

<margin><image-desc>manicule pointing right</image-desc> Red Lion <note>original: *Leo Rubens*</note></margin>
The red **oil** <note>original: *Oleum*</note> is now the **Red Lion** <note>original: *Leo* or *Leo Rubens*; a major stage in the Alchemical Great Work, symbolizing a powerful, fixed solar substance.</note>. # C.6. The Heavens Unlocked. <note>original: "Coelum Referatum"</note>

<margin>Fulminating gold</margin> <note>original: "Aurum fulminans"; a volatile gold compound used here as a starting material for a tincture.</note>
Regarding **gold-calx** <term>Gold-Kalck</term> <note>A metallic powder, usually an oxide, produced by chemical treatment.</note> then: dissolve your gold in a good **royal water** <term>Aqua Regis</term> <note>A powerful acid mixture capable of dissolving gold.</note>, which is made of equal parts **nitre** <term>Nitro</term> <note>Saltpeter.</note>, **vitriol** <term>Vitriol</term> <note>Sulfuric acid or a sulfate mineral.</note>, and **sal ammoniac** <term>Salarmoniac</term> <note>Ammonium chloride.</note>. When it is dissolved, precipitate it with a good **oil of tartar** <term>Oleo Tartari per deliquium</term> <note>A liquid made by allowing potassium carbonate to absorb moisture from the air until it liquefies.</note> until all the gold has fallen to the bottom. Wash the gold-calx well until it is "sweet" <note>Meaning washed free of all corrosive acids.</note>, and take one part of it to two parts of the **little birds** <term>Aviculæ</term> <note>Latin for "little birds," a symbolic name for the volatile white crystals or "flowers" described on the previous page.</note>. Grind these together and put them into a **retort** <term>Retorte</term> <note>A glass vessel with a long, curved neck used for distillation.</note>, and distill it in a **sand-bath** <term>Sand-Capelle</term>. You will then obtain a **liquid** <term>Liquor</term>, somewhat reddish like a butter. Take whatever has risen over and whatever has settled in the neck of the retort and add it back to the residue in the retort; apply heat through the **stages of fire** <term>Gradus Feuer</term> until it has flowed together. In this way, you have here a great **tincture** <term>Tinctur</term> <note>A concentrated alchemical substance used for transformation.</note> for gold, and also a good **medicine** <term>Medicin</term> for the human body. **Silver** is dissolved in **strong water** <term>Aqua fort</term> <note>Nitric acid.</note>, which is made from one part nitre and one part vitriol, and then precipitated with salt-water, washed, and treated exactly as was done with the gold. Thus, the **white tincture** is produced from it, and it **tinges** <note>Alchemically transforms the color and nature of a substance.</note> **mercury** <term>Mercurium</term> into the most beautiful silver.

C. 6

<margin>Mercury of the Philosophers <note>original: "Mercurius Philosophorum"</note></margin>
To make the **Mercury of the Philosophers** <term>Mercurius Philosophorum</term> <note>A specialized alchemical solvent or "spirit" believed to be the essential soul of metals, not to be confused with common quicksilver.</note>: Take one pound of honest **raw antimony** <term>Antimonium crudum</term> <note>Naturally occurring antimony sulfide (stibnite).</note> and half a pound of **nitre** <term>Nitrum</term> <note>Saltpeter.</note>. Grind these well together and place them both into a glowing **crucible** <note>A ceramic or graphite container used for high-heat melting.</note> so that the nitre ignites and begins to flow. Take care that no coals fall into it, so that no **regulus** <term>Regulus</term> <note>A metallic mass or "button" that settles at the bottom of a crucible; here, the author wants to avoid isolating the metal too early.</note> precipitates, but rather that everything together becomes a single **slag** <note>The glassy byproduct of smelting.</note>. Then, take it from the fire, crush it small while it is still warm, and mix into it an equal weight of **sal ammoniac** <term>Salarmoniac</term> <note>Ammonium chloride, used as a flux and to aid sublimation.</note>. Put it into a **sublimation vessel** <term>Sublimatorium</term> and sublimate it. The Mercury of the Philosophers will then rise up with the **sublimate** <note>The solid product formed when a substance is heated to a vapor and then cools directly back into a solid.</note>. This is far better than the one mentioned in the previous chapter, for it flows over a light just like water. When this is mixed with a **gold-calx** <term>Gold-Kalck</term> <note>A powdered form of gold, usually an oxide.</note>, it yields one of the most powerful **tinctures** <note>In alchemy, a "tincture" is a substance capable of "dyeing" or transmuting a base metal into a noble one.</note> for transmuting mercury into gold, or with silver, for transmuting mercury into silver.

### Note.

# C. 7. The Heavens Unlocked <note>original: "Coelum Reseratum"</note>.

Ensure that the external heat does not exceed the internal heat; therefore, maintain moderation so that your heat around the glass is not too great, for otherwise everything will be destroyed. Therefore, give it a fire that makes it alive: the true first degree, as hot as a **brooding hen** <note>A common alchemical reference to a gentle, steady warmth of approximately 100°F (38°C), necessary for the "incubation" of the matter.</note>. With such a constant warmth,

Proceed in everything as stated above until it returns to its perfection. For the longer the Tincture remains in the fire, the stronger it becomes, so that even one part of the mercury **tinges** a thousand parts into gold—or, if the Tincture is prepared for the "White" stage, into permanent silver.

Now follows how <term>Flamel</term> <note>Nicolas Flamel (c. 1330–1418), a French scribe who became a legendary figure in alchemy, reputed to have discovered the Philosopher's Stone</note>, <term>Artephius</term> <note>A celebrated medieval alchemical author, often claimed to have lived for over a thousand years using alchemical secrets</note>, <term>Pontanus</term> <note>John Pontanus, an alchemist famous for his "Epistle" concerning the "Secret Fire" necessary for the Great Work</note>, and others made the Stone; namely, how to make their mineral glue or their bitter water—as the true <term>Mercury of the Philosophers</term> <note>original: "Mercurium Philosophorum"; not common quicksilver, but a refined, spiritualized essence of metal</note>—from the ore of <term>antimony</term> <note>original: "Antimonii"; a brittle, silvery-white metalloid used as a primary starting material in many alchemical recipes</note>.

Take one pound of an upright [pure] <term>Hungarian Antimony ore</term> <note>original: "Minera Antimonii Hung."; Hungarian antimony was highly prized for its perceived purity and spiritual "potency"</note>. Take also one pound of <term>corrosive sublimate</term> <note>original: "Mercurii sublimati"</note> which has been sublimated from <term>vitriol</term> <note>original: "Vitriolo"; metallic sulfates</note>, <term>salt</term> <note>original: "Sale"</note>, and <term>quicksilver</term> <note>original: "Mercurio vivo"</note> in equal parts, as is customary. Grind these together quickly and put them together into a <term>retort</term> <note>a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids</note>, and distill it <term>by degrees</term> <note>original: "per Gradus"; gradually increasing the heat</note>. In this way, you will obtain a little <term>spirit</term> <note>original: "Spiritum"; a volatile, gaseous essence</note>; save this.

Put the remaining sublimate into an open glass bowl and place it in the open air. There, it will quickly draw the <term>world-spirits</term> <note>the "spiritus mundi," an invisible celestial influence or moisture believed to permeate the atmosphere</note> to itself. When you see that it wants to turn into a <term>liquid</term> <note>original: "Liqvor"</note>—which will not take long—put it back into a retort and distill the <term>bitter water</term> <note>original: "Aqua Pontica Amara"; a sharp alchemical solvent</note> over. For this living spirit gives life to all things and carries a special aerial nourishment with it. Pour this together with the spirit mentioned above into a large flask, and set it with a...

Take twelve **Loth** <note>An old German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to half an ounce or 15 grams</note> of this **solvent**, and pour it onto two **Loth** of **gold leaf**. The gold will dissolve in it just as ice melts in warm water. When five days have passed, pour more of the **solvent** onto it, after which the glass is stoppered and sealed; **digest** it again for five days, so that you gradually increase the heat; when the fire has gone out, give it a slightly stronger fire. ## Annotation.

Our author here arrives at a very important and secret work; most **philosophers** <note>early scientists or alchemists</note> have worked in this manner, <margin>Water of Paradise.</margin> and this is the true **Water of Paradise** <note>original: "Aqua Paradisi"</note>. **Flamel** <note>original: "Flamellus"</note> has called it his mineral <margin>Dragon's Tail.</margin> **Dragon's Tail** <note>original: "Cauda Draconis"; a symbol often representing the volatile and fixed parts of the matter</note>. It has a two-fold nature: one is fleshy, namely **Mercury** <term>Mercurius: the metal mercury, symbolizing the volatile spirit</term>, the other is fixed, which is **Antimony** <term>Antimonium: a metallic substance used to "purify" the other components</term>. It is the volatile <margin>Mercurial Water</margin> **Mercurial Water** <note>original: "Aqua Mercurialis"</note> of **Zacharias** <note>likely referring to Denis Zachaire, a 16th-century French alchemist</note>, which congeals in the cold and melts in the heat. Thus the author has withheld, first, the weight; one takes of **Antimony** one

# Chapter 9.

<margin>
Work from 
Antimony 
and Vitriol. 
Chapters on pages 61, 68, 73. 
256, 49. 
See the Key. 
<note>original Latin: "Opus ex Antimonio et Vitriolo. C. p. 61, 68, 73. 256, 49. V. Clavis."</note>
</margin>

Briefly, I must show you yet another secret work, namely how one can obtain the **Philosophers' Mercury** <term>Mercurius Philosophorum: the essential alchemical solvent or "first matter" required for the Great Work, not common quicksilver</term> in a short time. Take **Hungarian antimony** <term>Antimonii hungarici: stibnite or antimony sulfide ore, highly valued when sourced from Hungary for its perceived purity</term> and **native Hungarian vitriol** <term>Vitriolum nativum hungaricum: naturally occurring metal sulfates, likely iron or copper sulfate</term>, equal parts of each, ensuring however that all the stony mountain-rock is separated from both. Crush them both into an incredibly fine powder <note>literally "unbegreiflichen" or incomprehensible powder, meaning ground so finely it cannot be felt between the fingers</note>, mix them together, and place them into a sphere fired from good earth <note>a ceramic or clay vessel designed to withstand high heat</note>. Secure the opening with an iron stopper, well-sealed with **lute** <term>verlutirt: an airtight seal made of a paste of clay, lime, or other materials</term>. When it is dry, place it in a furnace so that you can apply fire from above for sixteen hours **by degrees** <note>original: "per Gradus"; a systematic increase in temperature</note>, until finally the sphere glows a dark red. In this way, its own **spirit** <note>original: "Spiritus"; the volatile or gaseous essence</note> will act upon its **body** <note>original: "Corpus"; the solid material</note>, opening it up and turning its inner nature outward. When the sphere has cooled, take out your **mass** <note>the reacted material</note>, crush it into small pieces, and place it in a flask. Pour a highly rectified **spirit of wine** <term>Spiritum vini: concentrated alcohol or brandy used as a solvent</term> over it and extract all the **soul** <note>original: "Anima"; the colored essence or "tincture" of the substance</note>. When no more can be extracted, pour all the extractions together and distill the spirit of wine away gently in a **water bath** <term>B. M. (Balneum Mariae): a "Mary's Bath" or double boiler, providing gentle, indirect heat</term>. Then the

## Note.

Concerning this work, your sphere must be made of strong **cast iron**, of such a size that it can hold at least two pounds of material. The hole in the sphere must be no larger than the thickness of a small finger, with a well-cut screw so that it can be closed tightly and well-**sealed with lute** <term>verlutirt: an airtight seal made of a paste of clay, lime, or other materials</term>. Place it in the furnace and proceed as the author stated. Afterward, take out your material and crush it into small pieces; put them in a flask and pour a good **vinegar** over it. This is because the **spirit of wine** <term>Spiritus vini: distilled alcohol or ethanol</term> is an **astral spirit** <note>a volatile essence believed to be influenced by or connected to celestial forces</note> and does not properly attack the **soul** <term>Animam: in alchemy, the "soul" of a metal refers to its sulfurous principle or its essential color and life-force</term>, but rather **precipitates** <term>præcipitirt: causes a solid to settle out of a solution</term> it. However, it does take up the **mercurial** <term>Mercuriale: the metallic principle of fluidity, weight, and vitality</term> part of the gold. When the vinegar no longer becomes colored by the material, pour all the vinegar together and distill it off again. Then, pour a **most highly rectified spirit of wine** <term>Spiritum vini rectificatissimum: alcohol distilled multiple times to achieve the highest possible purity</term> over it and extract it once more. Finally, pour all the **extractions** <term>Extractiones: the substances pulled out of the raw material by a solvent</term> together and distill the spirit of wine off once more,

<margin>Chapter 10.</margin> ...put [the water] into a **vial**, and add to it a purified **gold** that has previously been beaten into foil. This **liquid** <note>original: *Liquor*</note> will take into itself as much of the gold as it requires. Pour off the **liquid** once it has sat upon the gold for four days; by then, it has taken its **weight** <note>original: *Pondus*; in alchemy, this refers to the substance reaching its required density or "spiritual weight"</note> into itself. Into this **fountain** <note>The "Little Fountain of Bernhard" mentioned on the previous page</note> goes the **sun** <note>Alchemists used "the Sun" as the symbol and secret name for Gold</note>. Then, seal your **vial** and place it in such an **Athanor** <term>Athanor: a specialized alchemical furnace designed to provide steady, uniform heat over long periods, often called the "Pilosopher's furnace"</term> that you can provide it with only a dampened, gentle heat; otherwise, the pressure would shatter the glass into a thousand pieces.

The substance will become pitch-black, and in a short time, when you see that the colors are changing, increase the fire by one degree. Continue this until it has passed through all the colors and remains **fixed** <term>fixed: a state where a substance has become stable and no longer evaporates or changes when exposed to heat</term>. Then you can **augment** <note>increase the quantity or potency</note> your **tincture** with the remaining **liquid**, as will be explained more fully in the following writings.

This noble **liquid** has no equal, neither in the mineral nor the astral realms, for this "Great Water" consists of fire and exists through fire in all its parts. it is made from **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio*</note> and through **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note>. Such a secret path will not be easily found by any person, unless a good <meta>catchword: friend</meta>

## Chapter 10
<meta>Original signature: C, 10; Catchword: tel (refers to the start of the next page)</meta>

Friend, such a path has been opened to you, for you will find no recipe for this among any of the ancient philosophers; nor have the modern ones all known this path, because there are even more paths. <margin>The wet path of Basilius Valentinus <note>original: *Via humida Basilii Valentini*</note></margin> But if you wish to prepare the same according to the teaching of **Basilius** <note>Basilius Valentinus, a legendary 15th-century alchemist and Benedictine monk</note> in the **wet path** <term>wet path: an alchemical process using liquid solvents and slower, lower temperatures, generally considered safer but slower than the "dry path"</term>, then take note of the following secret instruction:

This is now also a very secret work, which the ancients also described very obscurely. Among others, **Basilius** mentioned it here and there in his *Twelve Keys*, and also in his other writings, and described it without any reservation, especially in his **sixth key**, namely: <margin>X</margin> A **double-fiery man** <note>Alchemical allegory likely referring to a potent mineral or "Mars" (iron) joined with "Antimony"</note> must be fed with a **white swan** <note>A symbol for purified white mercury or the "spirit" of the metal</note>; they must kill one another and subsequently be made living again. And as the words further sound with emphasis, they also call it **Azoth and fire** <note>original: *Azoth & ignis*; Azoth is the essential "mercury of the philosophers," believed to be the ultimate solvent</note> in the nearest way. **Pontanus** <note>Johannes Pontanus, an alchemist famous for his "epistle" on the secret fire</note> followed the **dry path** at the beginning, but it is somewhat more dangerous than this following method, as I have already informed you in the previous work, that the ancients in the dry path by means of **living sulfur** <note>original: *Sulphur vivum*; natural, unrefined sulfur</note> were thus remelted in its gray cement—

# C. 10. The Open Heaven.

...until it becomes like a flour <note>original: *wie ein Mehl*; referring to grinding the sulfur into a fine powder</note>. From this, take one or two pounds; put it into a stoneware pot that is wide and tall, and for every one pound of sulfur, pour in half a pound of **tree oil** <term>tree oil: typically refers to olive oil</term>. The pot must be tall because the sulfur rises greatly. Place it upon a charcoal fire so that the sulfur can be **opened up** <note>original: *aufschlüssen*; an alchemical term for dissolving a substance or making its inner virtues accessible</note> within the oil. Stir it constantly until all the sulfur has been opened up like a **red liver** <note>original: *rothe Leber*; likely a reference to "liver of sulfur," a name given to various potassium sulfides due to their liver-colored appearance</note>. Continue with the fire until it begins to become somewhat dry, like a **porous sponge**. 

Some perform this with **linseed oil** <term>linseed oil: oil pressed from the seeds of the flax plant</term>, but it stinks most horribly. One can also open it up with **walnut oil** <term>walnut oil: oil pressed from the meat of walnuts</term>. Then take this substance and put it into a glass retort that is well-coated <note>original: *beschlagen*; "lorication" or "luting," where the glass is covered in a protective layer of clay and sand to withstand high heat</note>. Place it in the furnace with a receiver attached and begin to distill **by degrees** <note>original: *per Gradus*; slowly increasing the temperature over time</note> for 16 to 24 hours. 

In the beginning, a milk-white water will pass over, which stinks powerfully. Finally, you will obtain a blood-red oil.

...with our **Azoth and Fire** <note>original: *Azoth & Ignem*; the universal solvent combined with the fiery principle</note>, and put them into a flask; also add 8 Loth <term>Loth: a traditional German unit of weight, roughly 14.6 grams or half an ounce</term> of a **Virgin Mercury** <note>original: *Jungfräulichen Mercurio*; mercury that has been purified but not yet "fixed"</note>, known as the **white swan**. Then take the glass and open it, and pour all the **phlegm** <term>phlegm: the watery, inactive part of a substance</term> off from the **precipitate** or **body** <note>original: *præcipitat oder Córper*</note>.

Tincture upon the **living mercury** <term>quicksilver; liquid mercury</term>, as it is now in itself; for of such a substance, only a few drops transform a whole pound of mercury into gold, which occurs as follows. Take one pound of **living mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii vivi*</note>, place it in a **crucible** <term>a heat-resistant container for melting metals</term>, and set it in the fire, in a **wind-furnace** <term>a furnace that uses a draft of air to reach the high temperatures necessary for smelting</term>; when it now begins to smoke, let only a few drops—two to three—fall upon it, then the mercury will receive a thick skin, for such water spreads itself over it; after ten or 12 minutes, let three drops fall upon it again; after a while, two more drops, and then blow strongly [with the bellows] and pour it out; then you have a **white gold** <note>likely a mercury-based alloy or amalgam that the author believes has the properties of gold but lacks the yellow color</note>, to which you can give the [true] color only with a good **ennobling water** <term>original: *Gradir-wasser*; a corrosive solution used to "exalt" or deepen the color of gold</term>, which is made from one part **vitriol** <term>sulfate minerals</term> and one part **nitre** <term>saltpeter</term> without a **receiver** <note>original: *Vorschlag*; in distillation, the vessel that collects the first vapors; here it may suggest a direct or simplified preparation</note>. ### Chapter 10. The Heaven Unlocked

...out and melt it with **borax** <term>a mineral salt used as a flux to help metals melt and to remove impurities</term>, then you have the most beautiful gold by all tests. But if you wish to work upon the great, ancient **Philosopher's Stone** <term>the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life</term>, proceed as follows:

Take your **Pontic** <note>original: *Pontisches*; likely referring to a "bitter" or "marine" quality, common in alchemical solvents</note> and **heavy-as-lead water** and divide it into two parts; regarding this, I wish to tell you more about this secret water. This water is as white as a teardrop; if one allows a drop of it to fall upon the hand, it does not wet the hand like other water, but runs together into a small droplet like oil. <margin>Old Saturn</margin>

One can create much benefit in specific ways <note>original: *particulariter*; referring to "particular" works in alchemy which aim at specific transformations or medicines rather than the universal "Great Work"</note> through this **Old Saturn** <note>original: *Saturnum*; Saturn is the alchemical name for lead, but in this context, it often refers to the raw ore or antimony</note>. <margin>Fire-stone of the ancients</margin>
pound into each, and drench this **Fire-stone of the ancients** <note>a poetic term for the mineral matter being worked upon, implying it contains the "inner fire" of nature</note> with the urine of a man who constantly drinks wine <note>alchemists believed the diet of the donor affected the chemical potency of the urine, which was a primary source of phosphorus and ammonia</note>. Set the vessel in the sun as soon as possible, but take care that it does not rain into it. <margin>Oil of Venus</margin>
likewise also **Venus** <note>the alchemical name for copper</note>. # 61 The Heavens Unlocked

<margin>Chapter 11.</margin>
<margin>Oil of Jupiter and Saturn</margin>
From **Jupiter** <note>original: *Jove*; the alchemical name for tin</note> and **Saturn** <note>original: *Saturno*; the alchemical name for lead</note> one can also prepare an **oil** <term>original: *Oehl*; in alchemy, an "oil" is a concentrated liquid extract or essence, not necessarily a fatty substance</term> in a similar manner; however, they must first be **calcined** <term>original: *calciniret*; reduced to a powder through heat or chemical action</term> with **sulfur**, only so much that they can be turned into powder. Then, moisten this with urine and set it in the sun until it turns into a juice, and further drive it over into an oil by means of a **retort** <note>original: *Retortam*; a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distilling liquids</note>.

### The Second Chapter.

<margin>The second vitriolic work. see pages 36, 49, 68, 73, 236.</margin>
Now I will also show you how to make a true **tincture** <term>a potent medicinal extract or a substance used for transformation</term> out of **vitriol** <note>original: *Vitriol*; a term for metal sulfates, usually iron (green vitriol) or copper (blue vitriol)</note>. See to it that you obtain a **Hungarian vitriol ore** <note>original: *Mineram Vitrioli hungaricam*; Hungarian vitriol was highly prized by alchemists for its perceived purity and high copper content</note>, and take filtered rain-water. Put the vitriol into stone jars that are narrow at the bottom and wide at the top. Pour the water over the vitriol to a depth of a good hand’s breadth; however, **May-dew** <note>original: *Mayen-Thau*; dew collected in the month of May was believed by alchemists to be impregnated with a "celestial spirit" or "world soul" that gave it special dissolving powers</note> would be better for this. Leave a hand's breadth of space at the top of the vessel. Divide this into several vessels and set them down so that no dust falls into them. 

<margin>Ruffles on the green vitriol</margin>
In this way, the pure essence will grow upward out of the vitriol and above the water, appearing quite ruffled like **butter** <note>original: *Butter*; a term used to describe a thick, waxy, or crystalline substance that "blooms" or precipitates out of a solution</note>. Take this off and put it into a clean vessel. Collect as much of this as possible until no more grows upward; save what remains behind until I tell you what you should use it for.

Take the fully grown **vitriol** <note>original: *Vitriol*; metal sulfates used as a base for many alchemical operations</note> all together, which will be like a **gur** <term>original: *Gur*; a soft, earthy mineral "juice" found in mines, believed by alchemists to be the "embryo" of metals before they harden into ore</term>. When no more will crystallize, take the remaining water and evaporate it until it becomes a **white salt**. # Chapter II. The Heavens Unlocked.

<sig>E 2</sig>

Put the stopper on it, and seal it shut by a lamp <note>glass vessels were often hermetically sealed by melting the neck over a flame</note>; place it in our secret furnace, and give it fire by degrees <term>original: *per Gradus*; a method of slowly increasing heat over time</term> for four months. Here you will see wonders, for the matter <term>original: *Materia*; the physical substance of the work</term> is not quite metallic nor quite astral. When the colors have passed in the final month and your matter has become fixed <term>original: *fix*; a state where the substance is stable and no longer evaporates or changes under heat</term>, then take the glass out, for the Stone is finished. If you project <note>the act of casting the medicinal powder onto molten metal</note> one part of this upon gold standing in flux <note>in a molten, flowing state</note>, it also becomes a Medicine. One part of that medicine upon a hundred parts of purified mercury <term>original: *Mercurii purgati*</term> likewise becomes a Medicine, and one part of that again upon a hundred parts of other metals.

If you wish to augment <term>original: *augmentiren*; to increase the quantity or the power of the alchemical substance</term> it, take your tincture <term>original: *Tinctur*; the concentrated essence or "Stone"</term> and grind it small, then pour two parts of its oil <term>original: *Oehl*</term> back onto four parts of the tincture. Seal the glass well and set it for two months to fix it by degrees; it will again pass through all the colors and become fixed. Note that you cannot augment with this tincture more than three times, for at the fourth time it becomes a penetrating oil, and thus you must stop. a salt. Grind this salt into small pieces and place it into a retort. Pour the distillate previously collected in the receiver back onto it and distill it off once more. ## Remark.

The author here at the beginning has bypassed the most important manual procedures. He states that one should take 6 pounds of "grown-out" <term>vitriol</term> <note>original: *ausgewachsenen Vitriol*; likely referring to vitriol that has been allowed to effloresce or form into large crystals</note> and place such into a small retort to drive the oil and <term>spirit</term> from it. However, the grown-out vitriol melts just like water, and thus does not work, for it penetrates all vessels. Mix an equal weight of charcoal dust <note>original: *Kohlgestübe*; powdered charcoal used to prevent the vitriol from frothing and to help it withstand the heat without melting into a liquid that leaks through the ceramic</note> into it; then the work will proceed well. Furthermore, he states in the process that one should take the salt that was extracted from the <term>caput mortuum</term> <note>original: *Capite mortuo*; literally "dead head," the solid residue left after distillation</note> and pour the oil onto it and distill them over together, which in a

## Annotation.

Take two pounds of a **Solar Mineral** <note>original: *Minera Solaris*; likely referring to gold ore or a specific variety of antimony associated with the Sun</note>, crush it small, put it into a flask, and pour 4 pounds of our **solvent** <term>Menstruum: a powerful liquid used to dissolve or extract the essence from a solid substance</term> upon it. # Annotation.

Dear God, if all the writings of this author had likewise been shared with the world, how all the **mysteries** <term>Mysteria: secret or divine knowledge accessible only to the initiated</term> would have fallen into misuse! And although he kept many practical steps secret, he has nevertheless laid so much from Nature before the eyes of the world that nothing more is left from which it could have become uncommonly fortunate. Therefore, you students of our **brotherhood**, if you receive this into your hands, keep it sealed within you; it is better to die than to reveal such things to wicked boys.

Now, to come to this work and to describe it in detail:
<margin>
The **Fifth Essence** of minerals.
<note>original: *Quinta Essentia mineralium*</note>
</margin>
the **Fifth Essence** <term>Quint-Essenz: the most purified and potent form of a substance, believed to contain its medicinal or spiritual core</term> can be extracted from the minerals in a short time: the red of **antimony** <term>Antimonii: a semi-metal used in alchemy for its purifying properties</term>, the sky-blue of **bismuth**, the grass-green of **lead-ore**, as shall be described one by one, since the author leads us toward them. The author intends for one to pour the **extractions** together after the **putrefaction** <term>Putrefaction: the breakdown of matter through gentle heat to release its internal components</term>, and to place them in a cellar so that they might form **crystals**; however, this does not work in that way. Instead, half of the **solvent** <term>Menstruum: a powerful liquid used to dissolve solids or extract their essence</term> must be drawn off over a very gentle fire in a **water bath** <term>B. M.: abbreviation for *Balneum Mariae* (Mary's Bath), a double-boiler used to heat substances gently and evenly</term>, then that which is distilled—

<summary>This page contains a moral warning to the alchemical brotherhood regarding the secrecy of their craft and provides technical instructions for extracting the "Fifth Essence" from various ores using a water bath.</summary>

## The 16th Chapter.

<margin>
*Tincture of Bismuth with the Water of the Fistful*
<note>original: "Tinctura Bismuthi cum Aqua Pugilli"</note>
</margin>
Take the <term>Bismuth</term> to make the <term>Tincture</term> <note>a potent alchemical extract</note> through the help of our secret <term>Solvent</term> <note>original: "Menstrui"</note>. Take 3 pounds of such, crush it small into an imperceptible powder, put it into a <term>Vial</term>, and pour as much of our <term>Solvent</term> upon it so that it stands 2 fingers high above the material. Now set the <term>Vial</term>, well-sealed, to <term>putrefy</term> for one month. After such time, the <term>Matter</term> will <term>dissolve</term> <note>original: "resolviren"</note> into a sky-blue <term>Liquid</term>. Pour this off and pour other <term>Solvent</term> upon it; let it <term>extract</term> again in very gentle warmth. Pour such <term>Extraction</term> with the previous one, and do this as often as until everything is drawn out. Then, pour all the <term>Extractions</term> together and place them in a cellar, so that stones <note>crystals</note> will form.

### Note

The author continues in the description of this work, but has concealed much regarding this process. One sees more wonders in no other work than in this very one, as it takes on all colors <margin>Chameleon</margin> like a chameleon. ### The 17th Chapter.

Furthermore, how to make and prepare the Philosopher's Stone from lead ore, according to the methods of the Greater and Lesser Peasant <note>original: "Bauers," referring to the pseudonymous alchemical author Rusticus</note> and Isaac Hollandus, is done as follows: Take three pounds of such [ore] and place it into a vial; pour our <term>Menstruum</term> <note>a solvent used to dissolve solids</note> over it until it rises one hand's breadth above it. # The Reserved Heaven

<margin>Chapter 18</margin> to reverberate <note>to reflect heat from the roof of the furnace back down onto the material</note>. **F.** is another furnace section where the **degree** <note>level of heat</note> is stronger. **FF.** is another opening where the degree is even stronger. **G.** is yet another opening for inserting the vessels, through which one can also stir the metals at the same time; for this reason, there are several openings here so that if one does not wish for the metal to reach a state of **flux** <note>a fully melted, flowing state</note>, one can move or swap the vessels. **H.** is an opening for simultaneously inserting a **crucible** <note>a ceramic pot for high-heat melting</note> to melt something. **I.** is the opening for building the fire. **K.** is the ash-hole. This furnace can be heated with coals, but it is better if it is heated with wood. **LL.** are the draft-holes; here one can **distill** and **sublime** <note>the process of heating a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again</note> at the same time, and in this furnace, one can also reduce the metals back into a **salty ash** <note>a metallic powder or "salt" resulting from prolonged heating</note>. The vessels are flat **shards** <note>shallow ceramic roasting dishes</note> made of good clay, half an inch thick. ...and when it is as red as a rose, one can test whether it dissolves in warm water, and whether all of its potency passes into the water while the metal falls to the bottom as a slimy white earth. If, however, this sign does not occur, then one must bring it to that state through lengthy <term>reverberation</term> <note>a process of heating a substance in a furnace where the flames are reflected or "reverberated" onto the material from the roof, rather than touching it directly</note>. In this way, one obtains a secret <term>vitriol</term> <note>a crystalline metal salt</note> of indescribable power. 

Should the metal, however, begin to clump together and <term>vitrify</term> <note>turn into glass; original: *vitrificiren*</note> during the warm <term>calcination</term> <note>heating to high temperatures to oxidize or break down a substance</note>, then it will not be easy to succeed in making a vitriol from it afterward, because its <term>principles</term> <note>the fundamental components or spiritual essences of a substance</note> are already determined toward <term>vitrification</term>. This glass <note>original Latin: *vitrum*</note> is the <term>final matter</term> <note>original Latin: *vltima materia*; the end of its physical evolution</note>, which—even through pounding, grinding, and reverberating—cannot be brought any further than to a white-grey ash. In this ash, the salt of metals <note>original Latin: *Sal metallorum*</note> still remains, which can be extracted with a vinegar. 

But if it has become glass, one can extract the <term>soul</term> <note>original Latin: *animam*; the volatile, essential essence</note> pure and beautiful, which is as good as that extracted from gold. Thus, one can obtain the red and white flower, as the body <note>original Latin: *Corpus*</note> and the soul <note>original Latin: *Animam*</note>, and these two can be united in an open fire to form the <term>tincture</term> <note>the ultimate alchemical medicine or transformative agent</note>.

## Annotation.

<margin>Metallic Vitriol. Soul of the Sun <note>original Latin: *Anima Solis*; refers to the spiritual essence or "tincture" of Gold</note>.</margin>
<margin>The true soul of metals.</margin>
Our author now comes forward and teaches how one should make a <term>vitriol</term> <note>a crystalline metal salt</note> out of metals, but he remains silent on this point: according to his teaching, it does not work well. One can certainly obtain a *glass* <note>original Latin: *Vitrum*</note> or a *salt of metals* <note>original Latin: *Sal metallorum*</note>, but not a true vitriol. However, if the sharpness of the sulfur remains with it—and one does not wash it away with water—and one <term>reverberates</term> <note>heats in a furnace where the flame is reflected onto the substance</note> the components together carefully, then one can easily obtain a vitriol from it. Furthermore, he mentions how one can make the *Soul of the Sun* <note>Gold</note> from the *glass*, but he also conceals the *method* <note>original Latin: *Modum*</note>.

The *glass* is crushed small into a delicate powder, placed in a flask, and the "Fencer’s Bath" <note>original: *Fechter-Bad*; a specialized alchemical solvent or "sharp water" described in the works of Basilius Valentinus</note> of **Basilius Valentinus** is poured over it. In this way, everything will be *extracted* from the glass, and it will remain behind as a white *earth* <note>original Latin: *Terra*</note>. All these *extractions* are poured together and the <term>menstruum</term> <note>solvent</note> is drawn off in a <term>water bath</term> <note>original Latin abbreviation: *B. M.* for *Balneum Mariae*; a gentle heating method using a vessel of hot water</note> until only a juice or a fine powder remains. This is then *extracted* again *with spirit of wine* <note>original Latin: *cum Spiritu vini*; concentrated alcohol</note>, and that same liquid is *distilled* again in the *water bath* until it becomes a juice or a red powder. Thus, one has the *Soul* <note>original Latin: *Animam*</note> or the entire *tincture* as sweet as sugar, derived from the whole metal;

# Chapter 20. The Heavens Unlocked.

<margin>
Red Sublimated Mercury, or Kunckel's Mercurial Secret, see *Laboratorium Chymicum*, Part III, Chapter IX, page 244. <note>Johann Kunckel (1630–1703) was a famous German chemist; this marginal note refers to his influential published works.</note>

...clever maneuvers can bring it so far that it rises up like blood, which afterwards with little effort can be made fixed and resistant to fire; it is done as follows: Take equal parts of **vitriol** <note>original: *Vitriol*; iron or copper sulfates used as acidic reagents</note> and **nitre** <note>original: *Nitrum*; saltpeter or potassium nitrate</note>, three pounds total, and one pound of **living mercury** <note>original Latin: *Mercurii vivi*; liquid quicksilver</note>, ground well together and driven up with a strong fire in a <term>sublimatory</term> <note>original Latin: *Sublimatorio*; a vessel used to heat a solid until it turns to vapor and collects again as a solid at the top</note>. Ignis internus.*</note></margin> for the **soul of vitriol** and **nitre** is within it, acting as the fire of the wise masters. This <term>tinctures</term> <note>original Latin: *tingiret*; to transform the color and fundamental nature of a metal</note> mercury into gold, 1 part to 1,000 parts.

### J. G. Toeltius, Chapter 20.

But to obtain a true **tincture** <note>a potent alchemical substance used for transmutation or healing</note> from such, the following work is necessary.

<margin>
See page 61.
NB: Johannes Bureki took, upon my instruction: coagulated mineral liquor of Mars <note>original Latin: *liq. min. mart. co-agulat.*; an iron-based solution</note>, Oil of Salzburg <note>original Latin: *Oli Juvaniensis*; likely a preparation from the Salzburg region</note>, in equal parts <note>original Latin: *aa*</note> 3 ounces and 9 drachms, and rubbed them together; thus, the **Gold-Sulfur** <note>a medicinal or alchemical sulfur of gold or antimony</note> drawn out from this **dead head** <note>the *caput mortuum* or residue left after heating</note> is preferable.
See page 69.
This succeeds after a few rounds of grinding in an iron mortar, so that it disappears and becomes a gray **mercury** <note>original: ☿</note> or, with the **gold** <note>original: ☉</note> under this mark, has given 1/4 mercury to gold, and everything that was in the other glass immediately received a blood-red, quite loose, tasteless **mercury** in the second action. July 1737.
</margin>

Take **Hungarian Vitriol** <note>a naturally occurring sulfate mineral, often containing copper or iron</note> that has grown out into crystals, and crystals of **Nitre** <note>saltpeter</note>, as much of the one as the other (3 pounds), and 1 pound of **virgin mercury** <note>naturally occurring, pure elemental quicksilver</note>. First, **calcine** <note>heat to a high temperature to drive off moisture</note> the **vitriol** and **nitre** slightly and grind them small, and mix the **mercury** into them, moistened and rubbed with a good **spirit of vitriol** <note>sulfuric acid</note> or **spirit of nitre** <note>nitric acid</note>. Otherwise, the **mercury** will not willingly unite with the salts. Then **sublimate** it <note>heat the mixture until it vaporizes and reforms as a solid at the top of the vessel</note>. Afterward, grind all the **sublimate** back into the **dead head** <note>the *caput mortuum* or solid residue remaining at the bottom</note> and sublimate it again. Finally, with a very strong fire, grind the **sublimate** back into the **dead head** once more and sublimate again with a strong fire, ensuring all joints on the **helm** <note>the cap or "head" of the distilling apparatus</note> and the **receiver** <note>the vessel that collects the distilled substance</note> are well **luted** <note>sealed with a specialized airtight paste or clay</note>. Then take out all the **sublimate** and separate it from the gray powder, and sublimate this **sublimate** three more times by itself. Then it will appear truly transparent red. Use only half of this and **fix** it <note>original: *figiret*; to make a substance stable and resistant to evaporation under heat</note> according to the author's teaching in a sealed glass, then **augment** <note>increase its quantity or potency</note> it with the other part, adding one part to four parts of the fixed **tincture**; or one can also use this red **sublimate**, when it...

## Remark.

It is true that no author has discovered all these secret paths as this one has, which is why the Brotherhood <note>Likely the Rosicrucians, an esoteric society focused on spiritual and physical transformation</note> was so convinced by it that they bought this *manuscript* right off the press for six thousand doubloons <note>An enormous sum of gold coins, emphasizing the perceived value of the secret</note>; a bookseller had indeed published it in a rogue-like manner, but almost not a single syllable was correctly arranged according to the *author's* intent. Now, however, to return to the above *process*: the author still keeps his "thumb on the scale" in every way <note>A German idiom suggesting he is still withholding the most crucial secret or "keeping it under his hat"</note>, yet no one has written like him since the world began. Therefore, note: as far as the **mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note> is concerned, it is correctly made, but his **oil of sulfur** <note>original: *Oleum Sulphuris*</note> is still much too corrosive; indeed, it produces a stone, but it does not do what the author describes. Instead, take your oil and put it into a flask, and pour a highly rectified **spirit of wine** <note>original: *Spiritu vini*; concentrated ethanol or high-proof alcohol</note> over it, so that it stands a hand's breadth above it; then, in an eye-

Distill the **spirit of wine** away from it with a very gentle fire, until a thick, fatty oil remains.

<margin>Elixir of Properties <note>original: *Elixir Proprietatis*; a famous medicinal formula attributed to Paracelsus, used for longevity and digestive health</note></margin>

A single drop of this elixir is as potent as a whole spoonful of the versions sold by today’s **charlatans** <note>original: *Vaganten*; itinerant quacks or unlicensed traveling healers</note>; indeed, their preparations are often more harmful than beneficial to mankind.

# J. G. Toeltius Chapter 23

...writes. Now I will also show you how you should transform this white stone into red, and also how to augment it <note>Augmentation is the process of increasing the quantity or the transformative power of the alchemical stone.</note>. Take your stone and grind it fine; place it in a vial and pour just enough of its distilled water over it so that it becomes moist and can dissolve. If you have 4 Loth <term>Loth<note>A historical German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to 14.5 to 17.5 grams.</note></term> of powder, pour 1 Loth of its water upon it. Three-quarters of the glass must remain empty of the matter. ### Chapter 25. The Heavens Unlocked <note>original: "Coelum Referatum." A common title in hermetic literature suggesting the revealing of divine or celestial secrets.</note>

...a private person should be accepted and received among us; however, as stated above, among hundreds there is hardly one who is capable of **divine magic** <note>original: "Magia divina." This refers to a spiritual or sacred form of alchemy and occult science rather than common sorcery.</note>, which teaches so much. If they only learn one or two tasks, they are satisfied; therefore,
<margin>History of the Brothers of the Rosy Cross <note>original: "Historia Fratrum Roseae Crucis."</note></margin>
the number of practitioners of magic—known as **Rosicrucians** <note>original: "Rosen-Creuzer." Members of a secret cultural and religious movement that claimed to possess secret wisdom passed down from antiquity.</note>—never increases beyond seven, even though, where possible, the entire brotherhood should consist of 77 persons according to the magical number. Thus we see that the spirit of the great **Jehovah** <note>The Hebrew name for God, often used in mystical texts to denote the divine source of all things.</note> must truly be at work among us; for as soon as one of them passes into eternity, the Spirit immediately reveals another, as has happened constantly among us for some six hundred years now. However, that our antiquity is attributed to **Christian Rosen** <note>Referring to Christian Rosenkreuz, the legendary 15th-century founder of the Rosicrucian Order. The author here argues the group is actually much older.</note> is completely false, as our ancestors already existed in the times of **Valerian** and **Diocletian** <note>Roman Emperors. Valerian reigned 253–260 AD and Diocletian reigned 284–305 AD; both were known for their severe persecution of Christians and scholars of the occult.</note>, the latter of whom lived in the year of Christ 284 and reigned for 20 years. Under this horrible tyrant, many holy men, both scholars and others, were forced to <term>retreat<note>original: "retiriren." To flee or withdraw to a safe place.</note></term> with their wives and children and travel to other countries; thus they first spread into France, subsequently into England, and from there to Holland.

<meta>This page continues the historical narrative of the Rosicrucian brotherhood, detailing a period of internal betrayal and dispersal during a time of war. J. G. Toeltii C. 25

...until they finally moved into Thuringia and Saxony; however, after the Roman War <note>Probably referring to the German Peasants' War (1524–1525), which involved the Holy Roman Empire, or perhaps the Schmalkaldic War.</note>, two of our brothers—through their own negligence and against their sworn duties—joined the rebels and supported them with a great deal of money. Consequently, when they were captured, they were fried in oil <note>A gruesome historical execution method often reserved for counterfeiters or those seen as particularly treacherous.</note>. Had they remained faithful to their duty, the vengeance of GOD would not have followed so closely at their heels. 

Under such circumstances, the brotherhood had to divide once again. Many retreated toward the East <note>original: "Morgen," literally "Morning," the direction of the rising sun.</note>, toward the South <note>original: "Mittag," literally "Midday," where the sun is at its height.</note>, and toward the West <note>original: "Abend," literally "Evening," where the sun sets.</note> with great sorrow in their hearts. Because of this, the brotherhood was split many times, so that they often had to seek one another out to reunite. All brothers were eventually sought out again after they were provided with signs of recognition <note>Secret passwords, gestures, or symbols used to identify members of the society.</note>. 

During this search, Friedrich Rose <note>A figure sometimes cited in Rosicrucian lore as a predecessor or relative of the legendary Christian Rosenkreuz.</note> was found among them because of his wisdom. This proves that our magical brothers and bishops descend from Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob. Anything else is merely a fiction <note>original: "Gedichte," which can mean a poem, a story, or a fabrication.</note> invented by those scholars who have been cast out from nature's true aim. We, however, can verify our descent year by year in our secret tradition.

### C. 25. Heaven Unlocked <note>original: *Coelum Reseratum*</note>

<margin>gold-Slime.</margin>
Now, to come to the author's **Gold-Slime**, we must testify before the eternal GOD that hardly a single soul among the brotherhood has known this **Matter** <term>Materia: the fundamental substance used in alchemical transformation</term>; therefore, from all corners of the world, like-minded seekers <note>original: "lieb-habende Personen," literally "love-having persons," referring to those dedicated to the Great Work</note> gather there and, with the greatest pleasure, remain quietly for half a year, waiting for one another. Grind these together, and put them into a **vial**, place it on our secret **Athanor** <term>Athanor: a specialized alchemical furnace designed to maintain a steady, uniform heat over long periods, often called the "Pigeoncote."</term> and let it pass through the **colors** <note>The sequential color changes (usually black, white, then red) that signify the maturation of the alchemical matter.</note>. 

When it is now **red** and **fixed** <note>A "fixed" substance is stable in fire and will not evaporate.</note>, and also flows like **wax**, then take the glass out and grind your **Stone** small. Grind 1 **Loth** of the **First Entity of the Moon** <note>original: "primo Ente Lunæ"; the purified essence of silver.</note> into it again, and let it pass through the colors once more. Repeat this seven times. When you have enough of the **First Entity**, you will obtain a mighty treasure, where 1 **Loth** of this, applied to 7 **Loth** of **Gold** in **flux** <note>The state of being melted or molten.</note>, turns it also back into a **red powder**. One **Loth** of this powder applied to seven thousand **Loth** of **Mercury** [transforms it] into **Gold**.

### Remark.

Here the author comes and points out a very short path; as far as his work here is concerned, it is correct and proceeds without hindrance. He has [gone] the path...

## Note

The author continues to reveal all the secret paths of the ancients, which he obtained from the **Monastery of Wolckenrieth** <note>Likely referring to the Cistercian Walkenried Abbey in Germany, known for its mining and metallurgical interests.</note>; for the monks there left this work entirely in figures <note>Symbolic diagrams or illustrations.</note> and also in secret writings. After it has been separated from the slag <term>slag</term> <note>The stony waste matter separated from metals during smelting.</note>, it should be sublimated <term>sublimated</term> <note>The process of heating a substance into a vapor so it collects as a solid again in the upper part of the vessel.</note> by itself in a strong, stoneware, and simultaneously glazed vessel—and this twice, each time for one hour.

After this, [sublimate it] also twice with the gold. The gold, after it has been precipitated with **Oil of Tartar** <term>Oil of Tartar</term> <note>original: *Oleo Tartari*. A fine powder of pure sulfur collected after sublimation.</note> and gently calcined <note>original: *ausgeglüet*. Heated to a high temperature until it becomes a fine powder.</note>. # The Conjunction

<note>The stage of "Conjunction" is the chemical marriage or union of two distinct substances—here, the "white" and "red" flowers.</note>

Take 4 *Loth* <note>A *Loth* is a historical Germanic unit of weight, approximately 15 to 16 grams.</note> of the **white flower**, and 1 *Loth* of the **red flower**. These should be ground until fine and delicate, then placed into a strong vial and well sealed with lute <term>verlutirt</term> <note>To "lute" a vessel means to seal the stoppers or joints with a specialized clay or paste to make it airtight and prevent the escape of precious vapors.</note>. Place this on a graded furnace <term>Grad-Ofen</term> <note>An alchemical furnace designed to provide specific, incremental levels of heat (degrees) over a set period.</note> for 10 days, adjusting the degrees of heat until the substance remains fixed and flowing <note>A highly sought-after state in alchemy where a substance is "fixed" (resistant to evaporation) yet "flowing" (it melts easily like wax).</note>, as—

### J. G. Tœltius
### C.3

then break the vial, grind the **tincture** <note>In alchemy, a "tincture" is a potent substance capable of "tinting" or transmuting base metals into noble ones.</note> very finely and delicately in a glass mortar, and add 1 **Loth** <note>A *Loth* is a historical Germanic unit of weight, approximately 15 to 16 grams.</note> of the **white flower** <note>A "flower" refers to a substance obtained through sublimation—the process of turning a solid into vapor and back to solid again.</note> to it. Place it back into a vial and let it pass through the colors <note>This refers to the *cauda pavonis* or "peacock's tail," a stage in the alchemical process where the material changes through various colors as it is heated.</note> for 30 days and nights. Then take it out again and grind 1 *Loth* of the **white flower** into it; let this pass through the colors for 20 days and nights. Then, in the same manner as before, add the **white flower** and let it pass through the colors for 15 days and nights; do this again and let it pass through the colors for 10 days and nights, then for 5 days, and finally for a seventh time for the last 5 days. However, you must always remove it from the heat [to add more flower], otherwise it will cause the glass to melt, as this **tincture** becomes very fluid.

Now, melt four *Loth* of gold, and while it is in flux, add 1 *Loth* of the **fixed tincture**. Let it flow for a quarter of an hour.

Now, melt 4 *Loth* of gold and add 1 *Loth* of this **tincture** <note>A potent substance capable of "tinting" or transmuting base metals into noble ones.</note> to it; 1 part of this tints 2,000 parts of silver into gold.

<margin>
**The Universal from the Particular** <note>original: *Universale ex Particulari*. This describes a process where a specific (particular) result is expanded into a universal alchemical agent.</note>
</margin>
Take 1 *Loth* of this **tincture** again, and grind into it 2 *Loth* of the **red flower** and 1 *Loth* of the **white flower**, adding them as before until the **Wheel of Nature** <note>A term for the cyclical process of heating, cooling, and circulating the matter in the vessel.</note> has turned 7 times. Then take 1 *Loth* of this **tincture** and melt 7 *Loth* of gold, and add the tincture into it; thus it becomes pure **medicine** <note>In alchemy, "medicine" refers to the most refined state of the Philosopher's Stone, which "heals" base metals into gold or restores human health.</note> and 1 *Loth* of this tints 7,000 *Loth* of other purified metals into gold; thus this work can be multiplied to infinity.

**Vegetable Stones** <note>original: *Lapides Vegetabiles*.</note>

**First Putrefaction. The 5th Key.** <note>original: *Putrefactio prima. V. Clavis.* In alchemy, "putrefaction" refers to the breaking down of a substance through gentle heat, often compared to the rotting of a seed before it sprouts.</note>

...[suit]able **flasks**, filling them halfway; seal them well and place them in a gentle **putrefaction** <note>A "gentle putrefaction" usually involves a constant, low heat, such as that provided by a warm water bath or a heap of fermenting horse manure.</note> for four weeks. A "Kanne" was a liquid measure that varied by region, but was typically between 1 and 2 liters.</note> of water, then 8 holes must be placed all around it, each as large as a pea. The vessel where the purified spirit is collected after it passes through the still-head.</note> to it, which must lie in water and have a narrow neck at the top.

(Balneum Mariae): a "Mary’s Bath," used for gentle, even heating by placing the vessel in boiling water</term> until a dry salt remains. Extract this once more with the phlegm so that if any **dregs** <note>original: *feces*; the solid sediment or impurities</note> settle again, they may all be removed. Pour all the extractions together once more and distill off all the phlegm until a little red powder, **sulfur**, or salt remains. You can also dissolve this once more in just enough of the above-mentioned **spirit** as is needed, and in a **phial** as a juice
