# The Heavens Unlocked.
## Chapter 31.

<margin>Star of the Sun, Soul of Wine <note>original: *Astrum solis, Anima vini*</note></margin>
The **Star of the Sun** <note>original: *Astrum solis*; likely a term for a perfected solar essence or gold-infused spirit</note>, as the true **Soul of Wine** <note>original: *Anima vini*</note>; when your **Alcahest** <term>Alcahest: a theoretical universal solvent in alchemy capable of dissolving any substance into its primary matter</term> has been united with the **Mercury** <term>Mercurio: alchemical quicksilver or the principle of fluidity</term> for the seventh time, bring this **Soul** <note>Anima</note> to it as well, and **distill** <term>destillire</term> them together from a **retort** <term>Retorte: a glass vessel with a long, downward-curved neck used for distillation</term>; in this way, you will obtain a treasure of great value, and can truly boast that you have climbed the highest step in **Chemistry** <term>Chymie: refers here to the "Great Work" of alchemy</term>.

But if you wish to work according to the author’s opinion, take the **Ambrosia** <note>A name given to a refined alchemical substance, often a medicinal salt or spirit</note>, as it is described here, but preserve the **vinegar** well. The **crystals** <term>Christallen</term>, however, are not yet pure; you must **extract** <term>extrahiren</term> them several times with their **phlegm** <term>Phlegma: the watery, inactive liquid remaining after spirits have been distilled off</term> and **recrystallize** <term>Christallisiren</term> them until they shine like a **ruby** and appear transparent. Place these into a small **retort**, attach a **receiver** <note>original: *Vorlage*; the vessel that collects the distilled liquid</note>, and **distill** its **spirit** <term>Spiritum</term> over from a **sand bath** <note>original: *aus dem Sande*; heating the vessel in a tray of sand to ensure a gentle, even heat</note>. When everything has come over and some **salt** <term>Saltz</term> has deposited itself, add it also to this **vinegar**. This is the true
<margin>Radical Vinegar <note>original: *Acetum radicatum*</note></margin>
**Radical Vinegar** <note>A highly concentrated and potent alchemical vinegar, often considered the "root" or essential form of the solvent</note>. The vinegar kept aside previously can be used for other things, though it is better if it is **conjoined** <term>conjungiret: united or chemically merged</term> with this. Our ancestors perhaps did not wish to have it included, yet it belongs there. These both being distilled over together, and from all
<meta>catchword: all</meta>

<summary>This page describes the final refinement of the "Soul of Wine" and its union with the Alcahest to achieve the highest level of alchemical mastery. It also provides instructions for purifying "Ambrosia" to create "Radical Vinegar," a potent solvent essential for the work.</summary>
<keywords>Star of the Sun, Soul of Wine, Alcahest, Radical Vinegar, distillation, crystallization, mercury, Ambrosia</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for distilling wine-based substances. On the previous page, the author was describing the purification of crystals using their own phlegm. This page explains the transition from those crystals to a "Natural Vinegar" and finally to a medicinal or alchemical "Oil" and "Salt" to be combined with gold.</meta>

# J. G. Toeltius, Chapter 31

...all the **phlegm** <term>Phlegma: the watery, tasteless part of a distillate</term> is separated, only then is it the true **Natural Vinegar** <note>original: *Natur-Essig*; in alchemy, this refers to a concentrated, reactive acid distilled from wine or minerals, not common table vinegar</note>. Take out the **salt** <term>Saltz</term> that remained behind in the **retort** <term>Retorte</term> and **dissolve** <note>original: *solvire*</note> it in the aforementioned **phlegm**, and **distill** <term>destillire</term> half of it away. Place the glass in a **cellar** <note>Alchemists used the cool, damp air of cellars to encourage salts to absorb moisture from the air, a process called "deliquescence"</note>, and no more **crystals** <term>Christallen</term> will form, which is a sign that the **Volatile Vinegar** <note>original: *Acetum volatile*</note> has been fully driven out. 

Allow it all to evaporate until only the **salt** remains; **calcine** <term>calcinire: to reduce a substance to powder by heating it to high temperatures</term> it until it becomes a proper **alkali** <note>original: *Alcale*; a fixed, caustic salt</note>. Afterward, place the **salt** in the cellar and let it flow into an **oil** <term>Oehl</term>. **Coagulate** <term>coagulire: to thicken or solidify a liquid</term> it again, and repeat this process six to seven times, until it no longer solidifies in the heat but remains fluid like an oil.

If one wishes to follow this path, one must observe this procedure in all works; however, it is very laborious and **sophisticated** <note>original: *sophistisch*; in this context, it means unnecessarily complex, tedious, or perhaps even a "blind" meant to distract the unworthy</note>, and it does not yield as much as the method described above. 

Then take two parts of your **vinegar**, put it into a **flask** <term>Kolben</term>, and pour one part of the aforementioned **spirit** <note>original: *Spiritu*</note> into it. In an instant, these will also turn into **salt**. Place this **salt** in the **ash-heat** <note>a sand or ash bath used for gentle, even heating</note> and **sublimate** <term>sublimire: to heat a solid until it turns into vapor and then solidifies again at the top of the vessel</term> it upward. Take this **salt** out of the **flask** very cleanly. When it weighs two **Loth** <note>a historical unit of weight; one Loth is approximately 14 to 17 grams</note>, grind half a **Loth** of **beaten gold** into it—or better yet, the **Purple Mantle** <note>original: *Purpur-Mantel*; likely a reference to a gold precipitate or "Purple of Cassius," a deep red or purple pigment formed by the reaction of gold salts with tin</note>—

<summary>This page describes the labor-intensive process of converting volatile vinegar and salts into a permanent alchemical oil through repeated deliquescence and calcination. It concludes with the instruction to combine the resulting sublimated salt with gold or a "Purple Mantle" preparation.</summary>
<keywords>Natural Vinegar, Volatile Vinegar, alkali, alchemical oil, sublimation, Purple Mantle, gold preparation, Loth weight, deliquescence</keywords>

<margin>Universal Solvent <note>original: "Menstruum Universale"</note></margin>
...can be used as a **universal solvent**, taking six parts of the aforementioned **sublimate** <term>Sublimat: a solid substance purified through heating into vapor and then cooling back into solid form</term> and one part of the **vinegar**, distilled together; all simple **essences** <term>Essenitien: the concentrated medicinal or spiritual "being" of a substance</term> may be extracted with it.

## Chapter 32.

<margin>The Stone from smoke or from the nature of the oak. See the key. <note>original: "Lapis e fumo sive ex natura qvercina. v. clavis."</note></margin>
The other **Stone** is made thus: Take fresh shavings of **oak wood**, fill a large **retort** <term>Retorte: a glass vessel with a long, downward-pointing neck used for distillation</term> with them, and attach a large **receiver** <term>Recipient: a vessel designed to collect the products of distillation</term>, 

<margin>Oak Vinegar <note>original: "Acetum Querninum"</note></margin>
and distill a **vinegar** from it. Make as much of this vinegar as is necessary. **Rectify** <term>rectificire: to purify a liquid by distilling it several times</term> it until it is sharp enough. Then, see to it that you obtain some shiny **chimney soot** from the chimney sweeps, ensuring that no filth is mixed with it. Take 10 to 12 pounds, divide it into various **flasks** <term>Kolben</term>, and pour enough of the vinegar into each flask so that the soot can be dissolved 

<margin>Philosophical Putrefaction <note>original: "Putrefactio Philosophica"; the alchemical stage of decomposition or fermentation through gentle heat</note></margin>
within it. Set this aside for a month to undergo **putrefaction** in horse manure <note>Horse manure was a standard laboratory tool used to provide a steady, gentle "blood-warm" heat for fermentation</note>, so that it will decay. Afterward, place the flask in sand, 

<margin>Phlegm <term>Phlegma: the watery, inactive, and tasteless component of a distilled substance</term></margin>
and begin to distill all the **phlegm** away from it. 

<margin>Burning Spirit <note>original: "Spiritus ardens"; a highly flammable, volatile alcohol or essence</note></margin>
Then it becomes a **burning spirit**. Rectify this, and you will obtain it pure and beautiful; but be careful that you do not come too close to it with any light,
